## Document 007: 10月通胀数据点评：PPI同比再创新高，结构有什么新的变化？

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月通胀数据点评：PPI同比再创新高，结构有什么新的变化？.pdf
Report source: 中证鹏元
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

研究发展部
李席丰
lixf@cspengyuan.com
主要内容：
10月 CPI 同比增长 0.7%，低于市场预期（0.8%），低于前值（0.9%）；
10月 PPI同比增长 5.7%，高于市场预期（5.4%），高于前值（5.0%）。
一、CPI：食品项拖累加大，对冲能源成本的上升
（1）食品项同比跌幅扩大，鲜菜鲜果和蛋类环比同步转跌，拖累加大，秋季
供应增加和天气改善是主因。猪价受限于猪周期磨底及需求修复的滞后，短
期难以转为 CPI 的正向拉动。
（2）非食品中，主要支撑力量仍来自高油价和 AI 涨价两大外生性输入变量，
内生需求延续低迷。服务价格环比转涨，来自季节性出行需求和政府主导的
医疗调价。家电、汽车等商品竞争激烈，价格以下跌为主，耐用消费品需求亟
待提振。黄金同比涨幅收窄，环比转为负向拖累。
（3）10 月终端商品与服务需求偏弱，基本对冲上游能源成本向下传导的动
能，带动 CPI 同比回落。需求端，短期制约有国补退坡、能源成本压力上升
等因素，中长期面临居民预期低迷和资产负债表受损带来的“主动去杠杆”。

成本端传导并非普涨，而是延续结构性分化。
二、PPI：结构上出现三个变化
（1）10月 PPI同比上涨 5.7%，较前值上升 0.7pct，创近四年新高，再次强于
市场预期；环比连续三个月上涨，涨幅较上月回落 0.5pct 至 0.7%。
（2）PPI 同比涨幅扩大，原油、煤炭和 AI 延续涨价主线，其中煤炭、石油相
关的能源化工链条对 PPI 同比的拉动由 2.8pct 扩大至 3.7pct；剔除能源链条
后，PPI同比由 2.2%回落至 2.0%。
（3）相比前月，PPI结构上出现三个变化：一是，油气链条的边际贡献由上
游转移到中下游；二是建材链条结构分化，形成地产拖累、基建托底；三是 AI
涨价持续，但是价格弹性收窄。
（4）利润层面，行业利润传导的裂口加大。PPI 采掘业（27.9%）→原材料
PPI 同比再创新高，结构有什么新的变化？
——10月通胀数据点评 宏观研究
2026年 11月 9 日

### Page 2

（11.6%）→加工（3.5%）→生活资料（-0.1%），采掘业到原材料增速差连续
9个月扩大，原材料到加工环节的增速差连续 3个月扩大，加工到生活资料的
增速差相对稳定。
三、展望：核心 CPI 存回落压力，PPI 同比 2027年中枢回落，二季度或加速
下跌
（1）通胀趋势：对于 CPI，PPI 的结构性上涨对 CPI 的拉动有限，同时在
实体预期低迷、总需求没有明显改善的背景下，后续 CPI 同比预计在 0-
1%区间运行，核心 CPI 存回落压力。对于 PPI，预计 11-12 月 PPI 同比在
5%左右的高位，具体增幅主要取决于油价中枢，但 PPI 环比涨速最快的
时间或已经过去。2027年一季度开始，PPI 面临持续上升的基数，价格中
枢预计震荡回落，二季度回落速度或加快。
（2）一揽子增量政策效果观察：资金转化存堵点和时滞，实物工作量形
成较慢，对建材链条终端价格的提振效果有限。总结看，可能来自两方面
因素：一是项目端的施工进度成为主要约束，堵点正从“资金下达”转向
“实物转化”；二是政策资金的投向不偏向传统建材，新兴产业单位投资

用钢用水泥强度远低于传统“铁公基”。专项债和资本金等政策资金的实
物转化时间不同。
（3）对债市的影响：短期信用债赎回风险快速修复，半年维度有经济压
力增加、资金利率中枢下移、长端债券供给减少、股市长周期调整等因素
支撑，四季度债市可能出现“抢跑”行情。30 年国债收益率按照预期向
2.0%靠近，未来一年有望到 1.9%。股市阶段底部较明确，11月迎周度级
别反弹，会修复短期风险偏好。

### Page 3

10 月 CPI 同比增长 0.7%，低于市场预期（0.8%），低于前值（0.9%）；
10 月 PPI 同比增长 5.7%，高于市场预期（5.4%），高于前值（5.0%）。
一、CPI：食品项拖累加大，对冲能源成本的上升
10 月 CPI 同比较上月下降 0.2pct，再次低于市场预期（0.8%）；CPI 环比持平，较上月回落 0.2pct，略
弱于季节性（0.12%）。分项来看，10月 CPI 内部分化再次加剧，食品项和非食品项同比增速差由-3.2%扩大
到-4.4%，食品项拖累加大。
剔除食品和能源后，10月核心 CPI 同比上涨 0.8%，较上月回落 0.2pct。今年春节错位扰动后，核心 CPI
同比围绕中枢窄幅波动，本月触及区间低点。核心 CPI 的成色受到金价这一输入性因素影响，剔除相关的
其他用品及服务项后，10月核心 CPI 为 0.75%，略低于上月。
1、食品项：同比跌幅由 1.7 %扩大至 2.9%，环比由上涨 0.4%转为下跌 1.0%，显著弱于季节性（0.02%）。

（1）猪肉：供应充足叠加节后需求回落，猪价结束连续三个月上涨，10月环比转为下跌 1.0%，同比跌
幅收窄至 8.6%。猪价前瞻指标显示：9 月能繁母猪存栏量为 3602 万头，是正常保有量目标（3750 万头）
的 96%，仍在调控的绿色区间（92%-103%）内。生猪加速去产能，但传导仍存时滞，猪价短期上涨动能尚
不足。11月以来猪肉价格小幅回落。
（2）其他：今年迎来暖冬，鲜菜、鲜果、鸡蛋环比均由涨转跌。其中蔬菜供应随秋季上市增加，鲜菜
价格环比由上涨 0.5%转为下跌 5.8%，低于季节性（2.3%）8.1pct，同比降幅由 8.1%扩大到 16.9%，连续两
月是食品项的最主要拖累；鲜果环比由上涨 0.6%转为下跌 0.6%，同比跌幅由 1.5%扩大到 3.8%；节后备货
需求减少，蛋类环比由上涨 2.0%转为下跌 5.2%；休渔期结束，水产品供应充足，水产品价格环比下跌 1.3%，
跌幅扩大 0.5pct。
食品项同比跌幅扩大，鲜菜鲜果和蛋类环比同步转跌，拖累加大，秋季供应增加和天气改善是主因。

猪价受限于猪周期磨底及需求修复的滞后，短期难以转为 CPI 的正向拉动。
2、非食品项：同比上涨 1.5%，环比上涨 0.2%，同环比涨幅均持平上月，环比略强于季节性（0.12%）。
（1）能源和金属：美伊冲突反复，地缘风险溢价推升原油价格。根据统计局数据，汽油价格上涨 22.4%，
涨幅比上月扩大 5.8pct，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.63pct，带动能源价格涨幅由上月的 7.3%扩大至本月的 9.9%。
黄金面临加息压力和资金分流，价格继续回落，黄金饰品价格同比涨幅较上月收窄 18.0pct，影响 CPI 同比
上涨约 0.02pct，环比转为负向拖累。两项输入性因素产生双向拉动，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.11pct。

### Page 4

（2）服务：10月服务价格环比由下跌 0.2%转为上涨 0.1%，基本持平于季节性（0.04%），拉动项来自
假期出行需求和医疗调价。国庆假期出行人数增加，宾馆住宿、飞机票和旅行社收费分别上涨 6.5%、3.3%
和 2.4%，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04pct。政府主导的医疗调价继续提供支撑，医疗服务价格环比连续多
月上涨。
（3）AI 消费电子：涨价沿产业链继续传导，10 月通信工具 CPI 环比连续 11 个月上涨，同比上涨 16.1%，
再次刷新有数据以来的历史新高。数据存储设备、平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机价格同比分别上涨 45.4%、
29.3%、23.0%和 16.7%，四项合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.26pct，较上月增加 0.03pct。
（4）补贴类商品：家用器具 CPI 环比连续 3 个月下跌，交通工具 CPI 环比连续 8个月下跌，居民大宗
商品价格竞争持续，汽车等部分行业产能过剩压力加大，持续向价格端传导。
（5）其他分项：毕业季后房屋进入租赁淡季，10 月房租 CPI 环比下跌 0.1%，弱于季节性（0.00%）。

房地产延续调整态势，贴息政策效果目前比较有限。
非食品中，主要支撑力量仍来自高油价和 AI 涨价两大外生性输入变量，内生需求延续低迷。服务价格
环比转涨，来自季节性出行需求和政府主导的医疗调价。家电、汽车等商品价格以下跌为主，耐用消费品
需求亟待提振。黄金同比回落，环比转为负向拖累。
10月终端商品与服务需求偏弱，基本对冲上游能源成本向下传导的动能，带动 CPI 同比回落。需求端，
短期制约有国补退坡、能源成本压力上升等因素，中长期面临居民预期低迷和资产负债表受损带来的“主
动去杠杆”。成本端传导并非普涨，而是延续结构性分化：国际油价支撑成品油，AI 产业链是主要亮点。

二、PPI：原油、煤炭和 AI 延续涨价主线 ，结构上出现三个变化
10 月 PPI 同比上涨 5.7%，较前值上升 0.7pct，创近四年新高，再次强于市场预期；环比连续三个月上
涨，涨幅较上月回落 0.5pct至 0.7%。PPI 同比涨幅扩大，原油、煤炭和 AI 延续涨价主线；煤炭、石油相关
的能源化工链条对 PPI同比的拉动由 2.8pct扩大至 3.7pct，剔除能源链条后，PPI同比由 2.2%回落至 2.0%。
相比前月，PPI 结构上出现三个变化：
变化一是，油气链条的边际贡献由上游转移到中下游。油价直接拉动石油天然气开采、石油加工等上
游行业，并在产业链上沿着“原油→成品油/乙烯→塑料/橡胶/化纤→终端工业品”的路径传递，每环节传导

### Page 8

递减且后端环节存在时滞。10 月，上游的石油开采和加工价格环比继续上涨，但涨幅收窄，而中下游的化
工、化纤、橡胶塑料环比涨幅均扩大，对 PPI 的环比拉动增强。另外，中下游化工品的涨价幅度逐级衰减，
依次为化学原料（+ 3.1%）、化纤（+2.4%）、橡胶塑料（+0.7%），越靠近终端消费，涨价力度越弱。
变化二是，建材链条结构分化，形成地产拖累、基建托底。10 月，黑色金属和非金属价格形成显著背
离，其中黑色金属采选和加工环比增速均回落，上游采选价格跌幅扩大 0.4pct 至 1.7%；非金属采选和加工
价格环比上涨，且涨幅扩大。具体看细分品种，约七成需求绑定地产竣工的浮法玻璃价格下跌，反映房地产
需求延续弱势，而同时绑定地产和基建的水泥价格上涨，一方面来自供给收紧和成本上升，另一方面来自基
建项目施工需求改善。9 月以来稳投资政策密集落地，两重项目和“六张网”建设加快，对水泥等非金属建
材形成支撑。不过多数工业品价格下跌，基建项目的拉动效果属于托底范畴。
变化三是，AI 涨价持续，但是价格弹性收窄。10月，计算机通信电子制造业价格环比上涨 0.1%，近两

个月涨幅回落，连续三个季度的 AI 涨价压力有所缓解；有色金属加工价格环比转负，反映 AI 算力金属的
短期需求预期在降温。AI 链条已经从供给紧缺、涨价驱动的第一阶段，进入产能放量、更关注现金流和盈
利兑现能力的第二阶段，2027年资本支出增速的回落或影响其未来涨幅。
根据统计局测算，价格上涨的行业中，煤炭开采、油气开采、石油加工、化学原料和加工、燃气生产、
通信电子、有色采选、有色加工、电气制造这 9 个行业，合计影响 PPI 同比上涨 6.14pct。相比于 9 月统计
的 8 个上涨行业，本次纳入燃气生产和供应业，其 10月同比涨幅扩大 2.3pct 至 7.9%。同比下跌行业，主要
在中下游制造业，如汽车制造业、医药制造业、酒饮料和精制茶制造业，降幅在 1.6%-5.2%之间，但降幅较
上月有收窄。
累计口径显示，居民消费端温和上涨，工业成本端压力明显上升。1—10 月 CPI 累计同比为 0.9%，连
续四个月持平，2025年全年累计为 0.0%；1—10月 PPI 累计同比上涨 2.7%，较上月扩大 0.4pct，2025 年全

年为下降 2.6%。国内通胀延续“CPI 低位温和、PPI 走势偏强”的格局，CPI-PPI 剪刀差再次扩大。
利润层面，行业利润传导的裂口继续加大。沿着产业链价格同比涨幅看，10 月，PPI 采掘业（27.9%）
→原材料（11.6%）→加工（3.5%）→生活资料（-0.1%），采掘业到原材料增速差连续 9个月扩大，原材料
到加工环节的增速差连续 3 个月扩大，加工到生活资料的增速差相对稳定。上游资源品涨价，但是中下游企
业受制于激烈的市场竞争和偏弱的终端需求，产品提价困难，价格传导在中下游明显受阻，同时企业利润被
严重挤压，上中下游利润分化加剧。

图表 12 PPI 分行业环比增速前五位、后五位

三、展望和债市策略
（1）通胀趋势：核心 CPI 存回落压力，PPI 同比年内在 5%左右，2027年中枢回落，二季度或加速下
跌
对于 CPI，食品项重点分类中，猪周期继续磨底，年底腌腊需求有提振效果，鲜菜鲜果受秋季供应增加
的影响；厄尔尼诺背景下我国冬季整体可能偏暖，但阶段性寒潮频发，这种温差剧烈波动对蔬菜的伤害更
大，鲜菜 CPI 预计在冬季部分月份超季节性上涨。鲜菜在 CPI 中的权重是 1.7% ，若鲜菜价格同比涨幅扩大
10%，大约直接拉动整体 CPI 上涨 0.17 pct。非食品项中，四季度出行类服务需求进入淡季，汽车、家用器
具等耐用消费品受制于消费需求，通信工具和医疗服务有望继续支撑，能源项取决于原油走势。PPI 的结构
性上涨对 CPI 的拉动有限，同时在实体预期低迷、总需求没有明显改善的背景下，后续 CPI 同比预计在 0-
1%区间运行，核心 CPI 存回落压力。
对于 PPI，能源驱动特征明显。其中，原油价格受地缘因素主导，短期面临供应受阻以及北半球冬季取

暖需求支撑；特朗普长期战争能力被削弱，但在过渡期仍有能力制造可控的地缘紧张，以增加谈判筹码和稳
定美债利率，四季度原油或呈高位震荡走势。10 月中旬到 11 月中旬，油价均值环比上涨约 4.9%。国内煤
炭供给偏紧，叠加冬季用煤需求，价格或保持偏强走势。有色金属价格边际走弱，AI 需求预期短期降温。
国内定价的黑色金属、非金属等工业品价格分化，中长期仍面临房地产下行的制约，但财政资金加速投放、
“六张网”建设等提供支撑。综合上述因素，预计 11-12月 PPI 同比在 5%左右的高位，具体增幅主要取决
于油价中枢，但 PPI 环比涨速最快的时间或已经过去。2027年一季度开始，PPI 面临持续上升的基数，价

### Page 11

格中枢预计震荡回落，二季度回落速度或加快。
（2）一揽子增量政策效果观察：资金转化存堵点和时滞，实物工作量形成较慢
9 月以来，一揽子政策相继发力，稳投资部署持续落地，8000 亿新型政策性金融工具、5500 亿元结存
限额和专项债等资金投放进度加快，但建材链数据显示实物工作量对终端价格的提振效果有限。总结看，可
能来自两方面因素：一是项目端的施工进度成为主要约束，堵点正从“资金下达”转向“实物转化”；二是
政策资金的投向不偏向传统建材。8000亿新型政策性金融工具大部分投向人工智能、数字经济等新兴产业，
“六张网”里算力网、新型电网权重上升，单位投资用钢用水泥强度远低于传统“铁公基”。另外，政策资
金的实物转化需要时间，其中专项债从发行、拨付到形成实物量，常见时滞是 1-3 个月；用作资本金的新建
项目分化明显，对于手续、配套融资存在短板的项目，资本金到用材有 1-2 个季度时滞，部分项目甚至更长。
这意味着 9月底集中落地的专项债、PSL 等基建资金，若以 2个月中枢时滞测算，实物工作量、钢材水泥等

建材需求的增量窗口大概率落 11-12 月；如果是新建项目，兑现窗口可能会拖到明年一季度。用于项目全周
期的 8000 亿新型政策性金融工具，更大规模的投资拉动效应将在 2027年及之后逐步显现。
（3）对债市的影响：债市反应钝化，30年国债未来一年有望到 1.9%
10 月 PPI 再次超出预期，但并未改变利率下行趋势，主要在当前债市核心驱动来自政策宽松和信贷收
缩，对国内通胀数据基本钝化。
展望后续，短期信用债赎回风险快速修复，半年维度有经济压力增加、资金利率中枢下移、长端债券供
给减少、股市长周期调整等因素支撑，基本面和政策面决定债市中期仍处于顺风期，四季度债市可能出现
“抢跑”行情。30 年国债收益率按照预期向 2.0%靠近，未来一年有望到 1.9%。债市核心风险来自拥挤的交
易结构，重点关注央行态度、资金价格，以及股市可能再次下跌带来的赎回冲击，期间调整 3-5BP 或仍是加
仓机会。股市阶段底部较明确，11 月迎周度级别反弹，会修复短期风险偏好。

## Document 008: 10月金融数据点评：需求从哪里来

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月金融数据点评：需求从哪里来.pdf
Report source: 中银证券
Date: 2026-11-15
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

宏观经济 | 证券研究报告 — 总量点评 2026 年 11 月 15 日

相关研究报告

20261112

中银国际证券股份有限公司 
具备证券投资咨询业务资格

证券分析师：张晓娇

证券分析师：朱启兵 
(8610)66229359 
Qibing.Zhu@bocichina.com 
证券投资咨询业务证书编号：S1300516090001

10 月金融数据点评

需求从哪里来   
10 月新增社融和新增信贷均低于万得一致预期，政府债融资对社融的贡献较
大，居民部门和企业部门融资需求都明显偏弱，新增信贷出现流向非银部门
的现象。 
 10 月新增社融 1.14 万亿元，较去年同期多增 3251 亿元，较 9 月少增 2.41 万亿元，
低于万得一致预期的 1.33 万亿元。10 月社融存量同比增长 7.2%，较 9 月上升了
0.07 个百分点，略低于万得一致预期的 7.27%。10 月社融口径新增人民币贷款少
增 5846 亿元，较去年同期少增 5692 亿元，较 9 月少增 1.86 万亿元。 
 10 月新增社融较去年同期多增，主要原因是政府债券较去年同期多增了 6983 亿
元，此外较去年同期多增的还有汇票、企业债、外币贷款和委托贷款；从当月新
增规模看，新增融资规模较大的项目，主要是政府债券 1.18 万亿元、新增企业债
融资 3446 亿元和委托贷款 1862 亿元，当月少增的是人民币贷款、汇票和信托贷
款。值得关注的是 10 月股票融资规模较 9 月明显下降，较去年同期也明显下降，
一定程度上反映出市场存在向下调整压力后，市场融资行为也出现了收紧。 
 从社融存量结构来看，2026 年 10 月与 9 月相比，占比上升的有政府债券（上升
0.20 个百分点）、企业债（上升 0.05 个百分点）和委托贷款（上升 0.04 个百分
点），占比下降的是人民币贷款（下降 0.25 个百分点）、汇票（下降 0.04 个百
分点）和信托贷款（下降 0.01 个百分点）。与去年同期相比，10 月占比上升较
多的是政府债券（上升 1.43 个百分点），占比下降较多的是人民币贷款（下降
1.47 个百分点）。低利率环境下，企业债融资规模占比持续上升。 
 10 月 M2 同比增长 7.1%，较 9 月下降 0.1 个百分点；M1 同比增长 4.4%，较 9 月
上升 1.2 个百分点；M0 同比增长 10.7%，较 9 月上升 0.1 个百分点。10 月活期存
款（M1-M0）环比上升 0.16%，定期存款（M2-M1）环比下降 0.29%，与去年同
期相比，活期存款环比增速较高，定期存款环比增速偏低。央行三季度货币政策
执行报告的专栏 1 中也提出，我国存款结构出现了诸如非银行业金融机构存款增
长较快、占比明显抬升，以及居民和企业存款呈现一定程度定期化的特征，但受
重要节假日、财政收支节奏等影响，居民、企业、财政存款会出现季节性波动，
因此应该更关注趋势性变化。 
 10 月关注非银存款多增。10 月金融机构新增存款 1.21 万亿元，多增的主要是非
银存款 1.58 万亿元和财政存款 1.40 万亿元，企业存款和居民存款均出现少增；
与去年同期相比，10 月新增存款多增 6000 亿元，其中财政存款多增 6800 亿元，
居民存款多增 4200 亿元。10 月财政存款多增规模较大，一方面是与政府债融资
规模较去年同期多增相对应，另一方面也与财政收支节奏有关，我们认为财政存
款多增为 2026 年四季度到 2027 年稳增长提供了资金保障；非银存款多增与居民
存款少增并存，我们认为虽然资本市场短期向下调整，但投资者对人民币股票市
场前景仍相对乐观。 
 10 月实体经济融资需求延续偏弱。10 月金融机构口径新增人民币贷款少增 1000
亿元，低于万得一致预期的 591 亿元。其中，新增短贷及票据少增 5717 亿元，
新增中长贷 37 亿元，企业贷少增 1600 亿元，居民贷少增 4300 亿元，多增幅度
较大的是金融同业贷 3470 亿元。我们认为 10 月信贷数据显示实体经济融资需求
依然偏弱，一方面是在较低的利率水平下，中长贷和企业债融资都对短贷构成挤
出，另一方面是全球经济前景存在不确定性，居民和企业扩张资产负债表的需求
明显下降。需要关注的是，金融同业贷规模明显上升，可能与证券、基金、保险
公司互换便利（SFISF）操作有关。 
 关注需求从哪里来。从三季度货币政策执行报告来看，未来较长一段时间之内适
度宽松的货币政策仍在，但我们认为 2026 年四季度至 2027 年最重要的问题是需
求从哪里来：从国内的角度看，居民新增贷款较去年同期少增的趋势延续，指向
居民消费和房地产销售可能持续疲弱，企业融资需求偏弱指向生产端扩张预期不
强，因此内需将延续依赖财政支出拉动的特点；从海外看，一方面主要央行仍在
加息通道当中，货币政策收紧可能导致需求下降，另一方面，美国中期选举后，
对 AI 的监管政策仍有一定变数，因此拉动全球经济上行重要动力的不确定性加
大。我们认为未来一段时间内，对金融数据的关注重点或不在于货币政策工具如
何调整，而在于实体经济金融环境保持平稳且适度宽松，建议重点关注利率水平
的波动。 
 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行；欧美经济回落速度偏快；国际局势复杂化。

观性的其他职务，没有担任本报告评论的上市公司的董事、监事或高级管理人员；也不拥有与该上市公司有关的任何
财务权益；本报告评论的上市公司或其它第三方都没有或没有承诺向本人提供与本报告有关的任何补偿或其它利益。 
中银国际证券股份有限公司同时声明，将通过公司网站披露本公司授权公众媒体及其他机构刊载或者转发证券研究报

公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

2026 年 11 月10 日

2026 年 7-9 月期 GDP 予測(最終版) 
～前期比年率▲0.4％の小幅マイナス成長を予想～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

11 月16 日に公表される26 年７-９月期の実質
ＧＤＰ成長率を前期比年率▲0.4％（前期比▲
0.1％）と予測する。10 月 30 日の段階では前期
比年率＋0.4％（前期比＋0.1％）と小幅プラス
成長を予想していたが、その後公表された経済
指標の結果を反映し、小幅マイナス成長へと予
測値を下方修正する。 
本日公表された26 年９月分の国際収支統計の
結果を反映し、26 年７-９月期の実質輸出を前期
比＋0.1％（従来予測値：同＋1.2％）へ下方修
正する。筆者の９月分の想定と比べてサービス
輸出が大きく下振れたことが主因である。実質
輸入も前期比＋3.0％（従来予測値：同＋
3.3％）と下方修正するが、輸出の下方修正度合
いの方が大きいことから、外需の予測値を前期
比年率寄与度▲1.9％Pt（従来予測値：同▲
1.3％Pt）へ下方修正している。 
また、９月分の家計調査と家計消費状況調査
が想定よりも下振れたことを反映し、個人消費
の予測値も前期比▲0.3％と、従来予測値の同▲
0.2％から小幅下方修正した。

物価高による下押しや耐久財需要の反動に加え、天候不順の影響もあって個人消費が減少する
ことが成長率を押し下げる見込みである。特に、８～９月には長雨・大雨や台風などの天候不順
が相次ぎ、外出手控えや旅行・宿泊の見送りなどを通じて消費が下押しされたとみられる。一
方、設備投資は高い伸びとなり、景気を下支えするとみられる。設備投資の伸びには特殊要因も

りません。
2/2
一部含まれているが、それを除いても堅調だったと考えられる。
７-９月期は４四半期ぶりのマイナス成長になると予想するが、マイナス幅は大きくない。今回
のマイナス成長には天候不順という一時的な下押し要因も含まれており、過去３四半期に高い成
長が続いた後であることや、企業部門の底堅さが維持されていることを踏まえれば、景気回復の
流れが途切れたと判断する必要はないだろう。
物価高による節約志向は根強く、個人消費の回復にはなお時間を要するとみられるが、設備投
資を中心に企業部門が好調さを維持している点は好材料である。家計部門が力強さを欠く一方、
企業部門が景気を下支えする構図のもと、日本経済は均してみれば緩やかな回復基調にあると判
断している。

## Document 130: 261110_daiwa_gce

Source PDF: all report pdf/261110_daiwa_gce.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review GCE’s 3Q26 results, released on 10 November.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 beat Bloomberg consensus. GCE’s 3Q26 
earnings came in at TWD6.39bn (EPS: TWD12.4), in line with our estimate 
and 3% higher vs. the street’s. While revenue was 7% lower than our 
expectation due to flooding in Thailand and product transition, gross margin 
expanded by 4pp QoQ to 38.7% mainly due to easing raw-material cost 
inflation and its own price increases. However, net income was impacted by 
higher non-op loss of TWD238m (likely due to FX loss) though offset by a 
lower effective tax rate of 25% as Thailand plant turned more profitable.

Business outlook. Looking ahead, GCE now guides for revenue to be 
flattish QoQ in 4Q26. We suspect that AMZN’s ASIC AI server gradually 
shifting its model from air-cooled (2 chipsets per mainboard) to liquid-
cooled (4 chipsets) and the year-end inventory-taking are the key reasons. 
However, we expect the stagnant revenue momentum to be short-lived and 
growth momentum to resume with new projects (including Google’s TPU 
and MSFT’s Maia) contributing from 1H27.

In 2H27, we expect new capacity ramps at its 2nd plant in Thailand and 2nd 
plant in Suzhou (acquisition of Pegatron’s land; across the 1st plant in 
Suzhou) to be growth drivers, which could further increase its allocation in 
major ASIC AI server projects. As for concerns over the thicker-HDI board 
(a combination of sequential lamination HLC and HDI together) that we 
highlighted in in our previous note (2Q26 results review: profitability missed 
slightly), we believe GCE has already overcome the difficulties for 3-
lamination HDI and can now successfully produce AMZN’s requirement on 
its Trn4 AI server heavy HDI board. However, GCE might still need to 
achieve the requirement for Google’s TPU v9 which requires c.5-6 
lamination techniques. We believe the capacity expansion in 2027 would 
not be enough to catch up with AI demand growth in 2027. As such, we 
believe it will further drive price hikes in CCL raw-material, CCL, and PCB, 
and see gross margin (GM) expansion over our forecast horizon. We raise 
our GM forecasts to 40.5%/41.3% (prior: 38.0/40.2%) for 2027-28E.

What we recommend: We raise our 2027-28E EPS by 1-10% to reflect its 
3Q26 results and higher GM on current supply-demand dynamics. We 
reaffirm our Buy (1) call but trim our 12M TP to TWD1,600 with a lower 
PER target of 21x (prior: 26x; vs. past-3-year range of 7-28x), on our 4-
quarter-forward diluted EPS, to reflect weaker market sentiment and on-
going concerns over HDI. Key risk: worse-than-expected AI server demand.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E earnings are 4-5% higher than consensus, 
likely due to our higher profitability assumptions.

10 November 2026

3Q26 review: results largely in line with our estimates

➢ GM expands with spec upgrade and favourable pricing environment 
➢ New client and new products likely to be key growth drivers in 2027 
➢ Maintaining Buy (1) rating; trimming 12M TP to TWD1,600

Company profile 
Founded in 1981 and listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 1998, Gold Circuit Electronics (GCE) was, 
in its early years, one of the world’s top-3 printed circuit board (PCB) vendors providing double-sided 
and multi-layer PCBs for notebooks. However, as the notebook market became saturated from 2011, 
GCE started reshuffling its revenue mix to more high-end products (servers, workstations and 
networking boards). The requirement of higher-layer PCBs from these products has not only proved 
GCE’s manufacturing capabilities but also created a high entry barrier. GCE’s revenue contribution from 
the server and networking segments has surged to over 80% in 2024 (from 37% in 2010), the highest 
among its PCB peers.

### Page 5

5

Gold Circuit Electronics (2368 TT): 10 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 10 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
GCE's Board consists of 9 directors, including 3 independent directors. The number of
independent directors is above Taiwan securities law's requirement of more than two and
exceeds one-fifth of the total number of directors on the board. The backgrounds of the
independent directors are diverse with expertise in the areas of business management, finance
& audit, and law, which should be positive for executive and board quality.
That said, the roles of CEO and chairman are held by one person, who is a second-generation
family member of GCE’s founder. The organisation structure appears to us to be a family
business, which could lead to conflicts of interest between key executives and outside
shareholders.
Capital management 2
GCE declared a DPS of TWD10.0 in 2025, another record high. GCE has declared cash
dividends for six consecutive years from 2020 to 2025, following a period of intermittent payouts
mainly due to fierce competition in the PCB industry (especially in notebook applications) and
high earnings volatility from its 3 plants in China caused by historically low utilisation rates. We
believe the company’s strategy to focus on high-end products (eg, servers and networking) has
started bearing fruit, which was also the main reason GCE was able to declare a record-high
cash dividend in 2025. Overall, we expect GCE to continue to benefit from ASP and gross-
margin expansion, driven by server and networking spec upgrades, which should be value-
accretive for investors in the long term.
Related party &
transaction 1 Sales to related parties were 0% of total 2025 sales and purchases from related parties were also 0% of total 2025 purchases. We see limited risk from related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 1
GCE is a multi-layer PCB manufacturer with major raw materials, such as thin core, copper foil,
pre-preg, copper ball, dry film, solder mask and various chemical materials used in the
manufacturing process. GCE has acquired the ISO 9001 and IECQ QC 080000 certifications to
improve its supply chain management capabilities in product quality and hazardous material
usage. Like most hardware vendors, GCE is also committed to refusing procurement of conflict
minerals and complying with the Responsible Business Alliance (RBA) Code of Conduct.
According to its 2025 annual report, over 99% of its top 40 suppliers returned their RBA
commitment letters. As part of its RBA compliance efforts, GCE holds an annual supply chain
meeting to review the achievement rate of its previous targets and set new goals for suppliers. In
2025, GCE audited 154 suppliers, including 114 on-site audits and 40 document reviews,
achieving a 100% completion rate against its audit targets. The company also evaluates new
suppliers based on ISO 9001, ISO 14001 and IATF 16949 certification requirements and
conducts annual supplier assessments.
S Data security 2
Information security is one of the key topics under the company’s risk management guidelines.
GCE constructed an information security system, under the “information security committee”, to
minimise the risk of information loss, sabotage, and leakage caused by human errors, malicious
destructions or natural disasters. Below are several measures the company has taken to
improve data security: GCE will conduct regular awareness training to ensure employees'
security awareness and fulfilment of information security system requirements. Also, the
information security committee will develop an information security continuity plan and conduct
drills to ensure continuous operation of the company.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 3

Investment Thesis and Price Target Risks and Methodology

Investment thesis - Hiwin Corp 
Hiwin is the second-largest global linear motion supplier with 18% market share in 2024. 
It continues to witness decelerating market share growth from 2020 when it started to 
pursue a deleveraging strategy.   
Although we expect Hiwin to continue to benefit from rising demand from the 
semiconductor/EV industry given the upgrading trend, we are Sell rated, given: (1) we 
expect a weak end-market demand recovery and high inventory levels for the near term, 
leading to high pricing pressure and a stagnant utilization rate; (2) continued 
overcapacity issues in mainland China, where the company has higher exposure (45% in 
2024) vs. peers such as THK (24%), and we expect this could lead to further 
deterioration in Hiwin’s revenue/profitability outlook in the near term; and (3) the 
negative impact from its long-term deleveraging strategy. Hiwin continues to pay back 
long-term debt and expand capacity to better compete with peers, which we believe will 
further impact EPS growth and valuation. The stock is currently trading above its 
historical average valuation (33.0x). We expect its P/E multiple to derate once the 
market begins to adjust for a prolonged downcycle and expect downgrades to 
consensus EPS growth/ROE estimates, suggesting high downside risk to Hiwin’s 
valuation.  
Price target risks and methodology - Hiwin Corp 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$180 is based on a target P/E of 20x 2027E 
EPS (in-line with Hiwin’s average downcycle valuation, or 1std lower than the historical 
average P/E for the past 10 years).  
Key upside risks: (1) Stronger-than-expected EV & semi CAPEX growth; (2) a favorable

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

We hosted Kinsus’ 3Q26 post-result follow-up call today (Nov 
10th). Key takeaways include: 
(1) Kinsus mentioned it is getting more market share from existing 
server CPU customers, AI GPU customer, and ASIC customers. 
Despite not having much market share gain opportunity in its AI 
server CPU products, the company mentioned the volume growth 
could still be 150% YoY in AI server CPU shipment in 2027 vs. 2026, 
suggesting a good revenue/GM growth outlook going into 2027/28.  
(2) Kinsus guides 2027/28 revenue to go up by at least 30% YoY 
each year while GM should be at least 35/40% in 2027/28, 
considering the orders/capacity/UTR/product mix differences, 
despite the key upside risk will be on like for like base pricing hike 
(the guidance above does not include any pricing hike assumption, 
but this could lift the revenue growth by another 15ppt in both 
years although not guaranteed, per the company). 
(3) For 4Q26, the company guided revenue to go up by 10% QoQ, 
with GM being expanded into 35% (from ~34% in 3Q26), despite a 
much more favorable than 3Q product mix and ~5-10% pricing hike 
on ABF & BT substrates. For 1Q27, the company expects some 
seasonal issues on consumer electronics and contact lens, and 
believes revenue should be flat to slightly up QoQ, despite possible 
GM slowdown during the low season. 
(4) In terms of the future investment plan, Kinsus mentioned it will 
only need to invest in the land/infrastructure/construction etc. while 
end customers will take care of the equipment; normally, equipment 
accounted for 60% of the total CAPEX while the rest only accounts 
for 40% of the total investment.  
(5) Kinsus mentioned it now holds ~70% of the AI server CPU 
substrate market share, and the company believes its market share 
should be pretty stable in this product cycle. For AI GPU, Kinsus

### Page 3

mentioned it expects its share to increase from 10% now to 30% in 2027 for its existing
customer’s supply chain. Kinsus also mentioned it is working on several ASIC customers’
substrates product, which could also be the key revenue/profit driver for 2027 and
beyond.
(6) In terms of the order forecast, the company mentioned it has not yet seen any order
cut or order delay on its ABF substrates business, but the company continues to
maintain its cautious but positive view on the overall substrate industry outlook.

Investment view
We are positive on Kinsus’ 3Q result (GM expanded by 7.8ppt QoQ in 3Q - see here), and
believe the company’s GM expansion trend will continue in coming quarters, despite the
company only guiding 1ppt QoQ growth on GM in 4Q (however, the company is also
giving a conservative guidance on profitability. Kinsus only guided GM to be 27-28% in
3Q26, but it ended up with 33.9% in its 3Q result). Moreover, we believe Kinsus’
proactive capacity expansion plan (1 new plant every year from 2025 to 2029) will start

to help the company to outperform peers from 2Q27 (the new K6-B plant starts
contributing revenue from 2Q27) which should also help the company to enjoy better
profitability in coming quarters, as the new plants will focus on high-end projects, whose
GM is generally 10ppt+ higher than the company’s average level).
Overall, we maintain our Buy rating on Kinsus, and we expect the better market share in
AI server CPU and its better position in the high-end ABF substrate industry to

strengthen into the long term.
We maintain our Buy rating with an unchanged 12m TP of NT$1,515, based on the
same 19.5x 2028E EPS, which is +2.0x STDV higher than Kinsus’ past upcycle average
P/E (14.2x).
Investment Thesis - Kinsus
Kinsus is one of the key IC substrates suppliers globally with ~10% ABF/BT substrates
market share in 2024, with ~18% of total 2025 revenue contributed by its contact lens
subsidiary, Pegavision (6941.TW, Not Covered). With our view that the ABF substrate

shortage started from 2Q26 (ABF substrate return to supply demand balance from late
2025), we believe Kinsus’ performance will continue to improve compared with high-end
ABF peers considering the company’s increasing market share in the high-end ABF
substrate market; however, ~36% of the company’s revenue was contributed by BT
substrates in 2025 and 30% of the company’s ABF revenue is from non-LTA customers,
which we believe will continue to enjoy a better pricing outlook. As a result, we believe

Kinsus’ earnings outlook should continue to be strong (~118% 2026-29E CAGR), mainly
driven by a better BT substrate and non-LTA ABF substrate pricing outlook, despite the
unfavorable demand outlook before 1H26. We believe Kinsus is an experienced
substrate supplier with a clear capacity expansion plan that can also upgrade its product
portfolio in the long term. We believe we are at the beginning of an industry upcycle,
making us positive on Kinsus’ stock performance over the next 12 months.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Kinsus
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,515 for Kinsus is based on
19.5x 2028E P/E, which is +2.0x STDV higher than Kinsus’ past upcycle average P/E

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

### Page 1

E Ink Holdings

Cuts 2026 guidance again; PTFE mass 
production in 2028; reiterate Neutral

Reiterate Rating: NEUTRAL | PO: 148.00 TWD | Price: 140.00 TWD

Cuts ’26 sales guidance to +9% YoY; IoT as key ’27 driver  
Mgmt. cut its 2026 guidance again and now expects overall sales to +9% YoY (prior: up 
10-15% YoY; Jan-Oct sales +4% YoY). It expects IoT sales up 35%+ YoY (BofAe: +39% 
YoY, with ESL sales +32% and signage sales more than doubling) and CE sales -15% YoY. 
Mgmt. cited weaker CE demand and still expects signage at single-digit% of 2026 sales 
(BofAe: 7%). The latest full-year guidance implies a mid- to high single-digit% MoM 
decline in Nov and Dec. Mgmt. gave no numeric 2027 guidance, but it still sees ESL and 
signage (especially mid-size) as the key drivers, while CE order visibility stays low. 
PTFE in focus on 3Q26 call; mass production likely in ’28 
E Ink’s PTFE collaboration with Taiflex (8039 TT; not covered) was one of the main 
topics on the 3Q26 call. We now take a neutral view on E Ink’s PTFE business. We see its 
strong R&D capability in material modification and strength in roll-to-roll manufacturing, 
but mgmt. said the business model is still under discussion and mass production could 
occur in 2028. As a result, our estimates currently do not include any PTFE contribution. 
3Q26 recap: less-favorable mix weighs on margins 
OP was 21%/17% below BofAe/consensus as GM/OPM of 52.2%/26.6% came in lower 
than expected (6.1ppt/7.0ppt below BofAe). Mgmt. said the weaker GM came from a 
higher mix of lower-margin module business. NP was 20%/15% below BofAe/consensus. 
Reiterate Neutral; cut PO to NT$148 on lower P/E 
We cut our 2026E EPS by 8% but lift our 2027-28E EPS by 0-5% to reflect 3Q26 miss 
and the latest guidance. We trim our PO to NT$148 (12x 2027E EPS) from NT$185 (15x 
2027E EPS), with a lower multiple on reduced CE demand visibility and macro risk. 12x is 
similar to ESL system integrators’ P/E average. We reiterate our Neutral given moderate 
sales growth, still-expanding margins, and fair valuation (Exhibit 4).

Company Sector 
Electronics

Company Description 
E Ink is top global e-paper supplier with over 90% market 
share. E Ink was originally spun out of MIT Media Lab in 
1997 and was acquired by Prime View International in 
2009. The two companies were combined/branded as E Ink 
into e-paper supply chain. Besides its headquarters in 
Hsinchu (Taiwan), E Ink has facilities in Linkou (Taiwan), 
Yangzhou (China), and South Hadley (US).

Investment Rationale 
We have a Neutral rating on E Ink given moderate 2026 
guidance and 2027 sales growth with potential 
demand/order risk due to component shortage and macro 
uncertainty. Yet we still like E Ink's dominant position in 
global e-paper market with over 90% market share, high 
margins thanks to higher mix from better-margin IoT 
business, and positive OCF/FCF.

Stock Data  
Price to Book Value 2.2x

OBAL RESEARCH

Price objective basis & risk 
E Ink Holdings (PVWIF) 
We derive our PO of NT$148 from 12x 2027E EPS. Our target P/E multiple is set at the 
average of global key ESL system integrators. It is also at the lower end of 11-34x P/E 
range since AI era from 2023. We believe E Ink's valuation has been re-set with a lower 
premium after AI as its business is less directly exposed to AI.

Upside risks: (1) stronger market demand in consumer electronics, (2) faster ESL 
demand/adoption, (3) faster adoption and more applications in various vertical markets 
like smart transportation/city and smart medical, (4) more favorable USD/RMB 
movement to drive margin expansion and/or non-operating items, and (5) higher non-
operating royalty income due to stronger TFT-LCD demand.

Downside risks: (1) much weaker-than-expected consumer demand, (2) slower-than-
expected ESL demand/adoption to replace from conventional labels, (3) lower utilization 
rate to weigh on margins under higher capex and depreciation costs, (4) higher raw 
material cost and/or logistics cost, (5) lower non-operating royalty income due to weaker 
TFT-LCD demand, and (6) unfavorable FX movement (USD/RMB and/or USD/NTD) to 
weigh on GM and/or non-operating items.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be 
secure) and 9 - pays no cash dividend. Coverage Cluster is comprised of stocks covered by a single analyst or two or more analysts sharing a common industry, 
sector, region or other classification(s). A stock’s coverage cluster is included in the most recent BofA Global Research report referencing the stock.

SK 하이닉스 (000660/KS) 
여전히 강력하다 
3Q26 영업이익 83 조원으로 예상 상회 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 83조원 (+38% QoQ)으로 예상을 상회했다. 메모
리 공급 부족 심화, HBM4 반영 시작에 따른 메모리 가격 상승이 환율 하락을 상쇄했
기 때문이다. 출하 DRAM +10%, NAND -1%, 가격 DRAM +28%, NAND 
+24%, 영업이익률 DRAM 82%, NAND 72%, HBM 판가는 HBM4 출하가 시작되
며 37% 수준 상승한 것으로 추정된다. 업황은 여전히 예상을 상회하고 있다.

4Q26 분기 영업이익 100 조원 시대 진입 전망 
4Q26 영업이익은 105조원 (+27% QoQ)으로 분기 100조원 시대에 진입할 것으로 
전망한다. 메모리 가격 강세가 지속되는 가운데, 1c 출하 가속화, M15X 램프업 본격화 
등으로 출하량 상승이 기존 예상을 상회할 것이다. 또한 HBM4 의 Cross-over 시작 
및 수율 안정화로 HBM 가격, 수익성 제고 동반이 지속될 것이다. 출하는 DRAM 
+24%, NAND +4%, 가격은 DRAM +8%, NAND +7%를 예상한다.

2027 년 영업이익 488 조원 (+70% YoY)으로 상향조정 
SK 하이닉스의 2027년 영업이익을 488 조원 (+70% YoY)으로 12% 상향 조정한
다. 원/달러 환율 전망치를 기존 1,340 원에서 1,315 원으로 하향 조정했음에도, 공급 
부족 심화 속 2027년향 HBM 가격 인상 및 예상을 상회하는 수율, 1Q27 진행될 모바
일 DRAM 가격 인상 등을 반영했기 때문이다. LTA 가격 역시 개별 약정된 범위 내 
시장 상황을 반영할 수 있는 구조라는 점을 감안하면, 지속 상승할 것이라 전망한다.

높은 주주환원 의지 확인, 높아진 실적 가시성, 지나친 저평가 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 4,000,000원을 유지한다. SK하이닉스는 LTA에 대해 기
존 5년에서 추가 연장에 대한 논의가 진행 중이며, 2029년까지 HBM, DRAM은 사실
상 완판, HBM 수요는 공급 가능 물량의 2 배를 상회, 2030 년까지 메모리 수요 우위 
국면 전망을 밝혔다. AI 인프라 투자의 강력한 명분과 메모리 수요의 구조성, 잠재적으
로 매우 높은 LTA 위반 비용을 감안하면 실적 가시성과 안정성 제고가 동반되는 국면
이다. 100 조원 수준의 주주환원은 2026 년 예상 FCF 의 66% 수준, 분기별 자사주 
매입, 소각 지속 계획을 통해 높은 주주환원 의지를 보여주고 있다. 실적 Duration 장
기화, 주주환원을 감안하면 12MF P/E 3.4X 수준의 현 주가는 현저한 저평가 국면이
다. 과도한 우려의 되돌림과 메모리 밸류에 대한 재논의 국면 진입을 전망한다.

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다.

## Document 318: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ November 16-20

Source PDF: all report pdf/ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ November 16-20.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-15
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 16-20
Date:    7 hours ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 16-20

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
November 16-20
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 14 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

Last week’s inflation data ran hot. October’s headline CPI rose 0.4% m/m,
while the core CPI rose 0.3% for a third straight month. The PPI for final
demand rose 0.3% m/m in October after a revised 1.4% in September,
lifting the y/y rate to 6.5%, the highest since November 2022.
This week’s calendar is busy, with the FOMC minutes, Nvidia’s earnings,
Anthropic’s IPO, retail sales, and industrial production. Here’s more:
(1) FOMC minutes & FedSpeak. Minutes of the October 27-28 FOMC

meeting (Wed) should show how many of the 19 participants (12 voters
and 7 nonvoters) leaned toward a rate hike. The Committee held the
federal funds rate (FFR) target range at 3.75%-4.00% in a 10-2 vote, with
Fed Governor Lisa Cook and Cleveland Fed President Beth Hammack
dissenting in favor of a 25bps increase.
Six Fed officials speak this week (Warsh, Williams, Musalem, Hammack,
Goolsbee, and Paulson). Fed Chair Kevin Warsh appears Monday at the

Fed’s AI and productivity task force hearing in the San Francisco Bay
Area. New York Fed President John Williams, who speaks Monday and
Wednesday, said on Thursday that “inflation is far too high” and that
further tightening will be needed. We think the Fed should and will hike by
50bps in December.
FFR futures imply 3.6 rate hikes of 25 bps over the next 12 months
including 2.6 over the next six months (chart). The odds of a 50bps hike at
the December meeting are just 11.5% currently.

### Page 4

The 2-year Treasury bond yield is at 4.86%, well above the 4.00% top of
the FFR target range, with 12-month futures at 4.78% (chart).
(2) Nvidia earnings & Anthropic IPO. Nvidia reports Q3 earnings on
Tuesday, its first report since Jensen Huang’s resignation as CEO.
Revenue is expected to be $109.1 billion, up from $57.0 billion a year
earlier and slightly above the company’s $108 billion guidance. The
stock’s forward P/E is down to 11.5 (chart). The conference call with

interim CEO Colette Kress, who remains CFO, is likely to be more
revealing than the numbers.

### Page 5

Anthropic shares are expected to begin trading Thursday, with a price
range of $70-$78. That implies a fully diluted valuation of $1.26 trillion to
$1.40 trillion. We feel confident about the company’s prospects based on
its S-1 filing.
(3) Retail sales. October retail sales (Tue) follow September’s 0.8% m/m
gain, with sales ex autos up 0.9%. Retail sales ex food services rose 6.2%
y/y in September, and Redbook same-store sales continue to run well

ahead of the official measure (chart).
(4) Manufacturing. November’s NY and Philly Fed surveys (Mon, Thu)
follow solid October readings. Their average edged up to 23.2 in October
from 22.7 in September. The average has run well ahead of
manufacturing production growth this year, which was just 1.4% y/y in

### Page 6

September (chart). Historically, the two have tracked more closely,
suggesting that factory output could accelerate in the months ahead.
October’s industrial production (Tue) follows September’s 0.7% m/m gain,
which lifted the index to 104.0. Capacity utilization was 76.9%.
Manufacturing aggregate weekly hours slipped to 84.0 in October from
84.2 in September, suggesting that factory output weakened last month
(chart).
(5) ADP & unemployment claims. ADP’s weekly employment reading

(Tue) will update a four-week average that has been rising for the past two
weeks (chart).

### Page 7

Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week’s 200,000, up from 189,000,
with the four-week average at 195,250 (chart). Continuing claims fell to
1,697,000 for the week ended October 30, with the four-week average
steady at 1,709,000.
💡

of the QuickTakes article. Paid members' contributions may be
featured in our segment, "Ed Answers Your Questions".

利、股債皆挫，顯示目前即使是AI、科技信

► 美國9月CPI年增率上升至3.7%，核心CPI年
增率由2.4%升至2.6%，核心月增率0.3%，
高於預期值0.2%。能源是通膨反彈的主因，
貢獻整體CPI月增幅超過一半；能源衝擊雖
為主導，但消費韌性使企業保有成本轉嫁能
力，住房與部分服務價格維持黏性，需求面
通膨略有提高，加上企業資本支出提升經濟
與企業獲利韌性，使總需求不易快速降溫。
► 凱投預期Fed 12月將升息1碼，明年3月再升
1碼，政策利率可能在4%以上維持更久。受
到油價高盤、通膨僵固，與財政惡化、政府
發債籌資逢企業債搶錢等影響，美歐日主要
國家公 債利 率近 期 來到高 位 。其中， 法國
2027年財政預算欲刪減支出(包括養老金、公
部門工資等)引發民眾反彈，衝擊主權信用評
等，法國CDS與法德10年期公債利差擴大、
歐元走弱，美元轉強，商品、黃金承壓。法
國政治不確定性讓歐股修正、法股最疲，成
熟國家公債殖利率升高，增加企業借貸成本
與股市評價壓力，續為金融市場擾動因子。

► 美國第三季財報季展開，市場將觀察企業在
高利率環境下的獲利表現。根據FactSet數據
顯示，標普500指數第三季獲利成長預估達
29.5%，顯示企業基本面仍具支撐，其中科
技 產業 受 惠AI需求帶動， 獲 利 成 長預 估達
65%，持續扮演企業獲利的重要成長動能。
► 聚焦科技類股，AI資本支出仍為本輪企業獲
利成長的主要驅動力。高盛指出，第三季AI

基礎建設相關企業將貢獻標普500獲利增長
的54%。此外，大型CSP之雲端業務營收有
望加速成長，反映AI運算及雲端服務需求持
續提升，有助支撐後續資本支出擴張動能。
► 近期市場波動仍受高利率環境影響，壓抑投
資人風險偏好與估值擴張空間，顯示市場目
前壓力更多來自評價面而非基本面。隨財報
數據陸續公布，若企業持續繳出優於預期的
獲利表現，並維持正向財測，市場有望逐步
回歸基本面評價，使企業獲利成長逐步支撐
股市表現。
美企財報獲利穩健，對股市中長期有撐

評價面疑慮下，股價難以進一步擴張

投資焦點
► 醫療保健、醫療服務與生物技術等行業，在
景氣不好時，市場預期民眾對醫療保健產業
的需求 不易 下降 ， 成為傳 統防禦 型類 股代
表。目前金融環境利率偏高，11月美國總統
大選國會恐變天，股市波動風險上升，觀察
過去當VIX恐慌指數上揚時，醫療保健產業
相較於科技股更穩健，具有更好抗震能力。
► 醫療與生技類股近一年漲幅明顯，主因1)生
技股過去一段時間受到高利率、融資成本及
臨床試驗風險壓抑，部分公司估值偏低。當
臨床數據改善、或導入AI篩選資料與藥物，
投資人重新評價未來現金流與價值。2)大型
藥廠面臨既有藥品專利到期壓力，透過收購
取得新藥與研發管線，提高有潛力的生技公
司併購價值，帶動整體產業估值回升。3)肥
胖、癌症與慢性病用藥創造長期市場需求，
創新藥物(如瘦瘦針)讓部分醫療公司成長不
再只依賴人口老化，高盛今年7月將全球減肥
藥市場2030年銷售額預估上調15%至1,140
億美元，看好此類藥品的長期商業潛力。
醫療生技近年表現佳，估今年3Q醫院客流量年增4%VIX恐慌指數上升時，健護醫療表現相較穩健

市場波動上升、醫療保健表現佳，獲利穩定成長

► 美國醫療費用深受法令影響，由於商業雇主
贊助保險(ESI)和平價醫療法案(ACA)的自付
額增加，以及最高自付費用要求，較去年同
期提高，使今年第三季美國整體住院人數、
手術量和醫院客流量均呈現改善。高盛預估
今年第三季醫院客流量年增4%，加上季節性
因素，下半年醫療保健利用率將較上半年成
長，整體第四季預估獲利穩健，甚至可延至
明年，反應在近期XLV ETF股價創新高。
► 展望2027年，各醫療保險計畫普遍優先考慮
透過提高會員保費，和要求費用分攤(尤其是
提高會員自付費用上限)來擴大利潤空間，並
持續優化覆蓋範圍和產品組合，預估商業雇
主提供的醫療保險(ESI)保費趨勢，將在明年
加速成長至過去20 年來的最高水準。 以
Refinitiv預估明年美國各產業獲利成長來看，
醫療保健和生技相較於今年獲利明顯增加，
反而今年獲利極佳的能源和通訊受高基期影
響，獲利增速下滑。投資人在整體金融環境
波動放大時期，宜以醫療生技股穩中求利。

股市
 美股第三季獲利年增率預估為雙位數成長，市場靜待財報結果與展望，和對AI資本支出回收影響。
考量地緣衝突、殖利率偏高、評價面壓力與鄰近美國期中選舉，短期金融市場紛擾不止，可增加防
禦股增強耐震度。長期AI、科技發展趨勢不變，逢股市拉回時可分批布局，分散配置於不同國家與
產業，降低市場整體波動，亦可以槓鈴策略(防禦+成長)穩中求利。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點，全球利率走揚有利銀行股。此外，地緣衝突頻傳，歐洲國防軍工仍
有投資題材。
策略風格：可增加防禦股配置，中長期看
好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分關注
醫療保健生技產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升，預估今年底前主要國家仍有升息空間，殖利率偏高環境使短中期債券提供吸
引力，逢利率走高、鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳，具穩定現金流與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後利
差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等

匯市
 歐洲政治風險上升、歐元短期弱盤，美元高檔有撐。
 日本銀行再度升息時間不定，日圓區間震盪。
美元高檔有撐
商品

### Page 17

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書(或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用(境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金(不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施)；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

## Document 351: LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI  investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

LatAm’s silicon dividend: 
tailwinds from the AI  
investment boom in the U.S.

1 
Authors 
Fancisco Campos 
Chief LatAm Economist

Beatriz Garcia Nunes 
LatAm Economist

October 28, 2026 
Key messages: 
- U.S. investment has broken away from Europe and China since 2023, 
powered by AI-related equipment, software and research spending. 
- The AI CapEx boom demands commodities and merchandise that LatAm 
can supply.  
- Latin America’s export gains are real but concentrated. Copper, computer 
and electronics products provide the clearest links to the U.S. AI buildout. 
- Chile is the region’s most direct copper beneficiary. Mexico captures the 
windfall stemming from increases hardware demand and trade-diversion. In 
these cases, the AI-related tailwinds have undergirded appreciable 
improvements in external accounts. 
- The cases for nickel and zinc illustrate that the dividend is not widespread. 
U.S. imports of the former have mostly returned toward pre-pandemic levels, 
while for the latter bilateral gains partly reflect supplier substitution. 
- While expanding AI use, supply shortages and incentives can extend the 
CapEx boom, its financial viability poses downside risks. 
Another ‘American exceptionalism’ story  
Investment has become one of the sharpest dividing lines among the world’s 
core economic blocs. Since 2024, U.S. gross fixed capital formation has firmed 
up appreciably. Meanwhile, investment in Europe has broadly stalled while in 
China’s it has retreated, the drivers of which are beyond the scope of this report 
(Figure 1).  
The U.S. divergence has largely been an AI story: a burst of spending on 
computing power, digital infrastructure and intellectual property has offset 
weakness in conventional construction and housing. Indeed, total U.S. fixed 
investment is nearly 12% above its 2023 level. Within it, investment in 
information-processing equipment has risen by roughly 57%, software outlays by 
about 35%, and research and development by around 15%. By contrast, non-
residential structures and residential investment sit below their 2023 levels 
(Figure 2).

### Page 2

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 2

Figure 1: The global investment divergence

Figure 2: AI-related categories lead U.S. investment

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Eurostat, National Bureau of Statistics of 
China, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Latin America’s commodity channel 
AI is not merely a semiconductor story. Chips require specialized minerals; data 
centers require electricity and cooling; electricity requires grids, transformers and 
storage. That chain pulls in copper, aluminum, silver, tin, lithium and selected 
semiconductor inputs, along with natural gas and uranium. Latin America has 
meaningful exposure to several of these inputs, but copper has emerged as the 
commodity with the cleanest and most economically significant regional link. 
Copper is used throughout the AI infrastructure stack, including power cables, 
substations, transformers, server boards, heat sinks and cooling systems. Rising 
U.S. copper demand has lifted imports from Chile and Peru, particularly the 
former (Figure 3). The effect matters beyond merchandise export volumes. 
Favorable terms-of-trade have helped Chile generate a current-account surplus 
for the first time in roughly fifteen years (0.2% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-
quarter rolling basis) and have allowed Peru to log the largest surplus in about 
forty-five years (4.5% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis).

28 October 2026

### Page 3

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 3

Figure 3: Copper epitomized the commodity link

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Not every metal is copper 
The broader mining story demands more caution, though. U.S. nickel imports have 
recovered toward pre-Covid levels, but the upswing has not displayed the same 
persistence as copper. Mexico has captured a disproportionate share of the 
bilateral improvement, yet that gain looks more like normalization than a 
sustained AI-driven break (Figure 4). Zinc tells a similar story. U.S. real imports 
stand shy of their pre-pandemic levels, and stronger purchases from Mexico and 
Peru partly reflect a reallocation away from Brazil rather than a decisive increase 
in U.S. demand (Figure 5).

Figure 4: Nickel: recovery, not a boom

Figure 5: Zinc: a substitution story

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

28 October 2026

### Page 4

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 4

Mexico’s hardware advantage 
The larger non-commodity prize is in computers and electronics. U.S. imports of 
these products have sky-rocketed, while the supplier mix has shifted away from 
China. Mexico and various Asian economies have gained share on the back of this 
double-whammy impulse (Figure 6). 
This helps explain why Mexico’s non-automotive manufacturing exports, notably 
electronics, have outpaced automotive shipments to the U.S. and pressures from 
higher oil prices. The resulting non-oil trade surplus has offset pressure from the 
oil balance and helped Mexico generate a balanced current account (0% of GDP 
as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis). The external boost is meaningful, 
although it should not necessarily be interpreted as bona fide evidence of 
nearshoring, in our view.

Figure 6: U.S. computer & electronics imports going into overdrive

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research.

How much runway remains? 
Quantifying or mapping out a timeframe for the AI investment outlook is beyond 
the scope of this piece or our expertise. Nevertheless, we’d venture to argue that 
the capex cycle appears more likely to mature gradually than to stop abruptly. 
One reason is that the investment case is shifting from frontier-model training 
toward enterprise inference. Capacity that once served development can 
increasingly run commercial workloads for companies. That creates a path to 
revenue even if artificial general intelligence (AGI) crystallizes later than 
expected. It also spreads demand to a much larger corporate customer base.  
A second reason is scarcity. Graphics processing units (GPUs) remain tight, while 
power availability, grid connections, cooling equipment, construction capacity 
and data-center complexity limit how quickly supply can respond. Bottlenecks 
may therefore extend the cycle. The constraint is especially relevant for copper 
28 October 2026

### Page 5

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 5

and power infrastructure, where projects have long lead times and permitting 
hurdles. 
A third reason is strategic. AI development resembles a prisoner’s dilemma at 
both the corporate and national levels. Each participant may worry about costs, 
safety and control, yet slowing unilaterally risks surrendering technological, 
commercial or national security advantages. The same tension is visible in calls 
for guardrails alongside continued spending. Competition can therefore sustain 
capex even when short-term returns are uncertain. 
We acknowledge the risks, nonetheless. Higher borrowing costs raise the hurdle 
rate for an infrastructure buildout financed partly through debt. More important, 
revenues must eventually justify the installed capital base. The bearish case is not 
that AI lacks value; it is that infrastructure spending may run ahead of 
monetization, compromising some companies’ repayment capacity. Public 
opposition to the development of AI infrastructure and/or adoption of AI 
technologies could also slow down the technology’s roll-out. 
The regional balance  
Summing up, the AI investment boom in the U.S. is delivering a dividend to parts 
of Latin America, not the region as a whole. Chile and Peru benefit through copper 
prices and export volumes. Mexico benefits through electronics manufacturing 
and the replacement of Chinese supply. For Brazil and Colombia, the AI dividend 
is rather muted. Where materialized, furthermore, the dividend supports trade 
and current accounts more clearly rather than improving the domestic 
investment. 
These dynamics should persist while U.S. AI investment remains supply-
constrained and strategically protected. If widespread adoption advances and 
the strategic race intensifies, the impulse can last. If financing costs rise further 
or monetization disappoints, the cycle may narrow. Either way, copper and 
Mexican electronics remain the region’s most visible gauges of the U.S. AI 
investment appetite.

28 October 2026

### Page 6

Deutsche Bank Research Institute

ITEM DEFINITIONS AND METHODOLOGY
3© 2026 LSEG.  All marks herein are used under licence. TRPR_82222_875
General:
All aggregates are bottom-up aggregates calculated from analysts' consensus forecasts unless otherwise noted. Bottom-up
aggregates are weighted calculations using the I/B/E/S data from LSEG share class monitored or the official index weight of
the index-provider. Top-down forecasts are compilations of strategists' estimates. I/B/E/S universe calculations are based on

all companies within a given market monitored by LSEG.
Benchmark index calculations, including those for official local market indices, the S&P 500 Index, and the FTSE World Equity
Indices are calculated using official index constituents and weightings. All index composites are representative of current
index constituents. Index constituents and weightings are updated monthly during options expiration (the Thursday before the
third Friday of every month) for every index except the S&P 500, the constituents of which are current as of the date of

calculation.
PEIGBY refers to the Price Earnings Inverse Government Bond Yield ratio, which measures equity valuation with respect to
the inverse-yield on long-term government bonds (now known as the 'Fed' model). All PEIGBY ratios are I/B/E/S universe-
specific except for the US ratio, where it is based on the S&P 500 Index.
Methodology:
For US and selected Canadian aggregates, calendar-year aggregation is performed using the sum of four quarters' estimates

and a quarterly pro-ration is applied to non-December year end companies' quarterly EPS estimates. Outside of the US and
Canada, all company-level data are calendarized prior to aggregation using the Compustat method of calendarization.
Aggregation is performed using the annual consensus EPS estimate and a May roll-back is applied to December year-end
markets. Aggregates for Japan, where most companies report in March, use an August rollback and a November rollback is

used for Australia and New Zealand, where the fiscal year-end for most companies is in June.
All 12-month-forward data for ex-US markets are calculated using a pro-rata weighting to annual EPS forecasts. The
weightings applied are a daily calibration out of 365 days relative to each constituent's individual fiscal year-end and the
current date.
All non-surprise data, including aggregate forecast earnings, forecast growth, and forward-looking valuation calculations are

weighted according to the I/B/E/S share class monitored for I/B/E/S universes or the weightings assigned to each constituent
in a given index. Aggregated earnings calculations are the equivalent of net profit aggregates, as they are derived from the
EPS forecasts aggregated to the net income level by the appropriate share weighting.
Earnings Aggregation:
For 12-month-forward earnings, the 12-month-forward EPS is used in place of the calendarized EPS in the formulas below.

I/B/E/S universe EPS Aggregate = Index EPS Aggregate =
Growth Calculations:
All growth calculations are the weighted year over year growth in earnings for the calendarized fiscal year period. The
formulas below use index shares for index growth calculations.
Calendarized Earnings Growth = 12-month-forward Growth =
Valuation:
All valuation calculations are the weighted-average price-earnings ratio for the fiscal period in question. The formulas below

use index shares for index P/E calculations.
Calendarized P/E Aggregate = 12-month-forward P/E =
Revisions Momentum:
Unless otherwise indicated, analysts' revisions momentum is a simple aggregation of the total number of estimates raised
divided by the total number of estimates lowered. Thus, any revisions ratio less than 1 indicates negative revisions
momentum while a ratio greater than 1 indicates positive revisions momentum, with 1 marking parity.

The Global Aggregates from LSEG product contains annual aggregates for universes included in this publication, including
supplemental aggregate data of over 100 items as well as history dating back to 1985 for North America and to 1987 for other
markets. To learn more about this product, contact your LSEG sales representative.
FTSE and the FTSE World Equity Indices are owned by FTSE International Limited.

## Document 401: Top of Mind - Assessing a Less Transparent Fed

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind - Assessing a Less Transparent Fed.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs (Top of Mind)
Date: 2026-08-25
Pages: 26
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh is steering the Fed into a less transparent era. So, is that
a positive or negative development, and what could it mean for markets and the
economy? We speak with Former Fed governors Donald Kohn and Stephen Miran
as well as GS’ Jan Hatzius who agree that rigid “Odyssean” forward guidance that
commits policymakers to a pre-set path risks policy mistakes and is therefore rarely
appropriate. But they somewhat disagree about the benefits of great er transparency

around the Fed’s reaction function. Hatzius and Kohn argue that such transparency
helps markets better anticipate and price in policy changes, accelerating monetary
policy transmission. But Miran believes that the line between committing to a path
and being transparent is clearer in theory than in practice. And while GS economists
and strategists find that reduc ed transparency leads to increased market volatility,
the question is whether this just increases the market’s noise or also improves the

market’s signal. Hatzius argues largely for the former and Miran for the latter.
Jan Hatzius, Chief Economist and Head of Global Investment Research, Goldman Sachs
“Markets price what they think the Fed will do, not what they think the Fed should do. That will remain true even if the Fed
obscures its reaction function… market guesses about the Fed’s next moves will simply become worse, leading to more
volatility... and importantly, volatility that serves no constructive economic purpose.”

Donald Kohn, former Vice Chairman of the Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System, Robert V. Roosa
Chair in International Economics and Senior Fellow, Brookings Institution
“I sympathize with Chairman Warsh to some extent. [But] the real problem with Warsh’s approach is his reluctance to articulate
how the FOMC sees the economy evolving… explaining the Fed’s thinking about the economic outlook is essential in helping
markets act as a stabilizing force and is a core component of accountability.”

ISSUE 151 | August 19, 2026 | 1:30 PM EDT

• No major changes in views.
Datapoints/trends we’re focused on
• The AI boom, which is driving large trade surpluses in North
Asia that are spilling over into stronger domestic activity.
• China’s property downturn; we find the housing drag on the
economy has diminished and should mostly fade by 2028.
• A historic opportunity for RMB bond internationalization,
though we think sustaining gains will require broader
structural changes.
• India’s macro conditions, which are showing signs of cyclical
improvement after a string of adverse shocks.

We provide a brief snapshot on the most important economies for the global markets

### Page 3

El
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 151

New Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh is steering the Fed into a less 
transparent era characterized by shorter post-meeting 
statements, less forward guidance, and a more limited role for 
projections, marking a meaningful break from the 
transparency revolution that has defined central banking over 
the last quarter century. So, what could a less transparent Fed 
mean for markets and the economy? 
For answers, we turn to former Fed governors Donald Kohn, 
now Robert V. Roosa Chair in International Economics and 
senior fellow at the Brookings Institution, and Stephen Miran, 
now senior strategist at Hudson Bay Capital Management, as 
well as Jan Hatzius, GS’ Chief Economist and Head of Global 
Investment Research. 
We first ask whether scaling back Fed communication is a 
positive or negative development. Hatzius says it’s likely 
negative, though he draws a clear line between transparency 
and forward guidance. He argues that clarity about the Fed’s 
reaction function helps markets better anticipate policy 
changes and quickly incorporate new information into financial 
conditions, thereby accelerating monetary policy transmission 
to the economy. So, he believes that unwinding that 
transparency would be a mistake. (Note: GS Global 
Economists Joseph Briggs and Megan Peters lay out the 
evidence backing this argument on pgs. 14-15.) 
But Hatzius takes a more nuanced view of forward guidance, 
arguing that rigid “Odyssean” guidance that commits 
policymakers to a pre-set path regardless of new information 
only makes sense at the effective lower bound for the policy 
rate, while conditional “Delphic” guidance tied to the 
economic outlook is generally more defensible. Although 
markets could confuse the two, he believes that most 
sophisticated investors understand the difference and is 
therefore not particularly worried that markets have become 
overly reliant on the policy dots published in the Fed’s 
Summary of Economic Projections (SEP). Instead, he 
considers them a “useful source of additional information 
about the Fed’s reaction function” (except for the long-run 
policy dot). If anything, Hatzius would prefer the dots move 
in an even more informative direction—by publishing each 
policymaker’s combined projections for growth, inflation, 
unemployment, and the policy rate on an anonymized basis. 
Kohn agrees that clarity about the Fed’s “narrative” and 
reaction function is vital. Explaining how the Fed sees the 
economy evolving and how policy would respond, he says, is 
“essential in helping markets act as a stabilizing force and is 
a core component of accountability.” Still, he sympathizes 
somewhat with Chairman Warsh’s view that the dot plot is 
“not helpful” (see pg. 9 for Warsh’s own words on this), 
arguing that the market’s hyper-focus on the SEP’s median 
policy dot “illustrates the risk of excessive precision and 
false certainty,” as small shifts in it can trigger unwarranted 
market moves. So, he has long favored omitting the median. 
Miran, though, is more supportive of Warsh’s push for a less 
transparent Fed. Like Hatzius and Kohn, he’s concerned that 
excessive forward guidance that locks the Fed into a policy 
path raises the risk of policy errors and also argues that such 
guidance can impair market pricing of risk. But he’s less 
convinced about the benefits of increased transparency 
around the Fed’s reaction function, making the case that the 
distinction between the Fed committing to a pre-set policy 
path and being transparent about its reaction function is clear 
in theory, but less so in practice. So, he thinks “the policy dots 
absolutely have to go” but feels less strongly about omitting 
the SEP’s economic projections. 
But would less transparency allow markets to provide the Fed 
with more “direct and unfiltered” information about the 
economy, as Warsh has argued? The answer seems to boil 
down to the implications for market volatility, and whether any 
resulting volatility would simply increase the market’s noise or 
also improve its signal. 
As for the volatility implications, the evidence is fairly clear. 
Briggs and Peters find that increased central bank 
transparency has led to a reduction in market volatility over the 
last several decades. This is consistent with GS Head of US 
Rates Strategy William Marshall’s recent observation of the 
reverse: that the US rates market has already priced 
substantial uncertainty associated with a less transparent Fed, 
with the July FOMC representing the largest non-cut meeting 
day surprise in almost three decades.  
But Marshall argues that whether such meeting-day surprises 
lead to structurally higher rates volatility depends (again) on 
whether the Fed’s reaction function is well understood. If it’s 
not, he would expect higher rates volatility to persist. GS FX 
Strategists Michael Cahill and Lexi Kanter find a similar 
pattern in FX: that central bank communication shifts can 
change which events and data drive FX volatility without 
necessarily raising it, but less transparency could increase 
Dollar volatility—all else equal (though global macro 
conditions remain its biggest driver).  
But the key debate seems to be whether the increased 
volatility resulting from less Fed transparency will improve or 
worsen the market’s signal. Hatzius is clearly in the “worsen” 
camp. He argues that markets price what they think the Fed 
will do—not what it should do—and less transparency won’t 
change that; it would simply produce worse guesses. So, he 
says that while volatility in and of itself is not necessarily bad, 
volatility driven by uncertainty about the Fed’s reaction 
function would serve no constructive economic purpose. 
Kohn agrees that the lack of a clear Fed narrative could create 
damaging uncertainty. But he argues that removing overly 
specific guidance could help free markets to process new 
information more independently and isn’t too worried about 
modestly higher volatility as a result. And Miran seems most 
convinced that reducing transparency would improve the 
markets’ signal, with volatility an unavoidable but acceptable 
cost: “The first best world of signal without volatility doesn't 
exist. I'd rather have that signal, even if it's messy, than 
throw the signal out by anchoring market expectations to the 
Fed's near-term projections.” 
Allison Nathan, Editor
